hypothesis $\tau>0$ is for: at $\tau=0$ neither $\kappa_W$ nor $Q$ moves
with the split and $\ell_0$ is constant rather than strictly concave.
**(ii)** is $(\star)$ substituted into Assumption 2’s $\ln g_W$, with
$\ln x_{u+1}=\ln P_u-\ell_u$ and $\ln z_{u+1}=\ln R_u-\ell_u$ and the
two $\ell_u$ terms collecting to $-m\ell_u$. **(iii)** Differentiating
$\mathcal{G}(\ln g)=\mathcal{P}$ twice gives
$\mathcal{G}'\cdot(\ln g)'' + \mathcal{G}''\cdot\big[(\ln g)'\big]^{2} = \mathcal{P}_{\phi\phi}$
with $(\ln g)'=\mathcal{P}_\phi/\mathcal{G}'$, which is (31);
$\mathcal{G}'=g\mathcal{H}'(g)$ is (22), and (32) is
$\mathcal{G}''=g\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{G}'/\mathrm{d}g$ with
$g\,\mathrm{d}\mu/\mathrm{d}g = -(1-\delta_G)\mu^{2}/g$ and likewise for
$\mu_K$. **(iv)** At $\delta=\delta_G=1$ both bracketed factors of (32)
vanish, which is the first clause; the second is a different statement
and is Proposition 16. **(v)** Write $u=\kappa_W$ and
$w=\alpha^{2}\tau(1-\phi)$. Then $u_\tau u_\phi = -w$,
$u_{\tau\tau}=u_{\phi\phi}=0$ and $u_{\tau\phi}=-\alpha$, so the Hessian
of $\ln u$ has determinant
$\big[w^{2}-(w-\alpha u)^{2}\big]/u^{4} = \alpha(2w-\alpha u)/u^{3}$,
and $2w-\alpha u = \alpha\big[\alpha\tau(1-\phi)-(1-\alpha)\big]$. Under
Assumption 1, $\alpha\tau(1-\phi)<\alpha$, so the bracket is negative
whenever $\alpha\le1-\alpha$. $\blacksquare$

Parts (i)–(iii) locate the relevant margins. The first date the proof
does not reach is $u=2$, because $-m\ell_1''>0$ is the first injection
of convexity that (30) allows; the tail is governed exactly by $(\ast)$.
Computation 9 examines both margins.

**Computation 9 (date-wise concavity at the reported cells, and its
limits).** *At ten profiles over eight cells, listed in Appendix C.9 —
six profiles at which the incumbent split is interior and four at which
it rests at its corner — the second difference of $\ell_u$ in $\phi$ is
strictly negative at every date $u\le799$ and at every proper $\phi$ on
a $61$-point grid, with a worst value between $-3.1\times10^{-3}$ and
$-1.1\times10^{-2}$ and agreement to seven significant figures across
step sizes from $5\times10^{-4}$ to $10^{-2}$. Where the split is
interior the worst date is $u=2$ at four of the six, as (30) predicts;
at the other two the worst value is the converged tail, at the last
audited date and the top of the split grid. $V_W$ is concave in $\phi$
on the same grid at all ten. The **capitalist’s** half of the same
hypothesis is a different object under (O) — the concavity his clause
needs is of $\ln P_t$ in his rate, not of any population — and it is not
measured here. It is measured in Computation 12, on an interval of rates
rather than at a point, which is what Theorem 7 asks for and what this
computation’s capitalist clause did not supply even before the objective
changed. Criterion $(\ast)$ holds at the six interior cells with a
margin between $4.9$ and $1.2\times10^{3}$.*

*Neither condition is a universal, and both fail inside the assumptions
rather than outside them. At $\alpha=0.175$, $\gamma=0.746$,
$\theta=0.358$, $\delta=0.034$, $\delta_G=0.342$, $A=1.362$ with
$\tau=0.34$, $i=0.41$ and $\phi=0.6894$ — a profile whose rest point is
interior and stable to six decimals across simulation horizons from
$10^{3}$ to $1.2\times10^{5}$, with $g=0.96657$ satisfying both
Assumption 4 and properness — $(\ast)$ fails and
$\mathrm{d}^{2}\ln g/\mathrm{d}\phi^{2}$ is $+6.766177\times10^{-3}$ by
(31) against $+6.766176\times10^{-3}$ read off the simulated path’s
tail, and $+6.766\times10^{-3}$ again from a third route that solves the
rest point directly and second-differences $\ln g$ there, positive at
all five step sizes and agreeing to $3.4\times10^{-6}$ relative — three
computations sharing no step. The date-wise condition fails there from
date $87$, where the discount weight $\beta^{u}$ is still
$2.9\times10^{-2}$; the gap between that condition and Theorem 7’s is
therefore wide, and it is worth measuring rather than asserting. Theorem
7’s own weaker hypothesis survives very much longer: the second
derivative of $\ln N_{W,t}$ is negative at every $t\le1189$, rising
toward zero at the rate (31) fixes, and first turns positive at
$t=1190$, where the discount weight $\beta^{t}$ is $8.0\times10^{-22}$.
So the counterexample is exact and quantitatively negligible at once,
and it is stable across step sizes from $5\times10^{-4}$ to
$2\times10^{-3}$. At that cell $V_W$ is concave in $\phi$ regardless,
with no sign change in its second difference on a $47$-point grid — the
expected relation between a sufficient condition and its conclusion.*

What that counterexample costs is worth stating exactly, because the
proof above uses less than the theorem asks. Concavity of $\ln W_{j,t}$
enters only to make each summand of (29) convex, and for $\sigma>1$ that
summand is convex precisely when, with derivatives taken in the
deviating instruments $s$ on $I$,
$$\nabla^{2}\ln W_{j,t}\;\preceq\;(\sigma-1)\,\nabla\ln W_{j,t}\,\nabla\ln W_{j,t}^{\top},$$
which is the per-date form of the condition Section 8.7 writes as
$\lambda_{\max}(\mathsf{M},\mathsf{G})\le\sigma-1$, and reduces to
$(\ln W_{j,t})''\le(\sigma-1)\big((\ln W_{j,t})'\big)^{2}$ when one
instrument is varied. The two fail at opposite ends of the horizon.
Concavity must fail eventually, because the second derivative of
$\ln N_{W,t}$ inherits $t\,\mathrm{d}^{2}\ln g/\mathrm{d}\phi^{2}$ and
that is positive at this cell; the displayed condition must hold
eventually, because its allowance grows like
$t^{2}(\mathrm{d}\ln g/\mathrm{d}\phi)^{2}$. At the cell above the
displayed condition holds at every date computed, and at the crossing
its allowance is $65$ against a second derivative of $5.5\times10^{-4}$.
The counterexample is therefore to the hypothesis as stated and not to
the conclusion. Computation 13 measures the same wedge from the other
direction and at other cells; what is added here is that the
counterexample this section exhibits does not reach across it.

Theorem 7 asks for curvature at every date, and its hypothesis as stated
is false. For the worker there is a second route that asks for no
curvature at all, and it turns on a change of parameterisation rather
than on a bound. His instruments reach the system through exactly three
coefficients,
$$\kappa \;=\; (1-\alpha)+(1-\phi)\tau\alpha, \qquad \varpi \;=\; \phi\tau,
  \qquad \iota \;=\; i(1-\tau), \tag{33}$$ and through nothing else:
$\kappa$ is his consumption share, $\varpi$ scales the flow into the
public stock, $\iota$ scales the flow into capital. These are the three
direct log-impacts of Theorem 4’s Step 3, read as parameters rather than
as perturbations. The tax also moves the capitalist’s consumption, but
$c_C$ reaches neither stock nor $N_W$, so the worker’s payoff sees no
fourth channel.

**Proposition 14 (the fixed-start margin keeps its form).** *Let
Assumptions 1–3 hold with $\theta\in(0,1)$, fix a start, and let the
constant shares be proper along the path they generate from it. For the
derivative statements assume in addition that the per-date share
elasticities $\partial\ell_u/\partial s$ are bounded uniformly in $u$
along that path, which local stability supplies whenever the start lies
in the basin of an interior rest point; the identity itself needs none
of this. Write $F(\kappa,\varpi,\iota)$ for the worker’s discounted
payoff along that path, so that
$V_W(\tau,\phi,i) = F\big(\kappa_W(\tau,\phi),\,\phi\tau,\,i(1-\tau)\big)$
identically — not to first order, and not only at a rest point. Let
$\mathcal{A}_\kappa=\partial F/\partial\ln\kappa$,
$\mathcal{A}_\varpi=\partial F/\partial\ln\varpi$ and
$\mathcal{A}_\iota=\partial F/\partial\ln\iota$, which do not depend on
which instrument is varied. Then
$\mathcal{A}_\kappa,\mathcal{A}_\varpi,\mathcal{A}_\iota>0$
unconditionally, and with the **fixed-start wedge**
$\Omega^{\rm fix} := \theta\mathcal{A}_\varpi\big/\big[(1-\theta)\mathcal{A}_\kappa\big]>0$,*
$$\frac{\partial V_W}{\partial\phi} \;=\; \frac{\mathcal{A}_\kappa}{\theta}\,\Psi^{\rm fix}(\phi),
\qquad \Psi^{\rm fix}(\phi) \;=\; \frac{(1-\theta)\,\Omega^{\rm fix}(\phi)}{\phi}
   \;-\; \frac{\theta\tau\alpha}{\kappa_W(\phi)} , \tag{34}$$ *which is
(6) with $\Omega$ replaced by $\Omega^{\rm fix}$. Hence
$\operatorname{sign}(\partial V_W/\partial\phi)=\operatorname{sign}\Psi^{\rm fix}$
from every start. At a start equal to the induced rest point, (20)
forces $\mathcal{A}_\varpi/\mathcal{A}_\kappa=(1-\theta)\Omega/\theta$,
so $\Omega^{\rm fix}=\Omega$ and Proposition 14 reduces to Theorem 4
exactly.*

*Proof.* The identity is read off the primitives: $C_W=\kappa Y$, the
public law carries $\varpi\alpha Y$, the capital law carries
$\iota\alpha Y$, and $V_W$ depends on the shares only through the path
of $N_W$, which those three laws generate. Neither $\nu$ nor $g_C$
enters $x'$, $z'$ or $g_W$. That much is an identity between functions
and holds at any state whatever. Differentiating it in a share is a
further step, and it is not the one Appendix A.4 takes: A.4
differentiates in the *state*, and its domination is local, resting on
the spectral radius Theorem 2(iii) supplies at an interior rest point
rather than on properness. In a share the argument is the same in shape
and cheaper in substance. With
$\partial\ln N_{W,t}/\partial s=\sum_{u<t}\partial\ell_u/\partial s$, a
uniform bound $M$ on the per-date elasticities gives
$|\partial\ln N_{W,t}/\partial s|\le Mt$, so the differentiated series
is *eventually* dominated by a multiple of $t\,\tilde\beta'^{t}$ for any
$\tilde\beta'\in(\tilde\beta,1)$, the finitely many earlier terms being
finite, and that is summable. The eventually is not a courtesy: the
weights are $\beta^{t}N_{W,t}^{1-\sigma}$, which equal
$N_{W,0}^{1-\sigma}\tilde\beta^{t}$ on the balanced path and not off it,
so the domination is what the boundedness hypothesis above buys and is
why that hypothesis is stated rather than properness alone. The linear
growth in $t$ is the price of a permanent rather than transitory
deviation.

The basin clause of the hypothesis is an argument and not an appeal, and
it is worth writing out because the far-field unit root Section 7
records appears to threaten it. Write $\xi_u=(\ln x_u,\ln z_u)$, so that
the constant-share transition is a smooth map $\xi_u\mapsto\xi_{u+1}$ on
the open orthant — an interior rest point forces $\varpi>0$ and
$\iota>0$, and those keep every iterate interior from an interior start
— and write $\mathcal{J}(\xi)$ for its Jacobian, the matrix of Theorem
2(iii) when $\xi$ is the rest point. The sensitivity
$\mathsf{D}_u=\partial\xi_u/\partial s$ obeys
$\mathsf{D}_{u+1}=\mathcal{J}(\xi_u)\mathsf{D}_u+\mathsf{f}_u$, with
$\mathsf{f}_u$ the derivative of one step in the share, continuous along
the path. If the start lies in the basin then $\xi_u\to\xi^\ast$, and
Theorem 2(iii) makes $\rho(\mathcal{J}(\xi^\ast))<1$: in a norm adapted
to $\mathcal{J}(\xi^\ast)$ there are a date $U$ and a $\bar\rho<1$ with
$\|\mathcal{J}(\xi_u)\|\le\bar\rho$ for every $u\ge U$. Beyond $U$ the
recursion contracts and its forcing converges, hence is bounded, so
$\sup_{u\ge U}\|\mathsf{D}_u\|\le\|\mathsf{D}_U\|+\sup_u\|\mathsf{f}_u\|/(1-\bar\rho)$;
before $U$ there are finitely many dates, each contributing a finite
term. So $\sup_u\|\mathsf{D}_u\|<\infty$, and
$\partial\ell_u/\partial s=\nabla\ell(\xi_u)\cdot\mathsf{D}_u+\partial\ell/\partial s$
is bounded uniformly in $u$: $\mathsf{D}_u$ by the bound just given, the
gradient and the direct term by continuity along a convergent interior
path. Nothing ties the split to the point $s$: the contraction
neighbourhood and $\bar\rho$ serve every share near $s$ at once, the
rest point moving continuously, and the date $U$ survives small changes
of the share because it is reached through finitely many smooth
compositions — so the bound is locally uniform in the share, which is
what differentiating the series term by term asks. The far-field unit
root is not contradicted but located: as the start recedes, $U$ and with
it the bound grow without limit, so the argument yields no bound uniform
over starts — and the proposition claims none, fixing its start before
differentiating anything. The chain rule with
$\partial\ln\kappa/\partial\phi=-\tau\alpha/\kappa_W$ and
$\partial\ln\varpi/\partial\phi=1/\phi$ then gives (34) once
$\Omega^{\rm fix}$ is named. For the signs, write the transition in
levels: $K'=(1-\delta)K+\iota\alpha AK^{\alpha}N^{1-\alpha}$,
$G'=(1-\delta_G)G+\varpi\alpha AK^{\alpha}N^{1-\alpha}$ and,
substituting $c_W=\kappa AK^{\alpha}N^{-\alpha}$ and $z=G/N$ into
Assumption 2,
$N' = B(\kappa A)^{\gamma\theta}K^{\gamma\theta\alpha}G^{\gamma(1-\theta)}N^{\,1-m}$
with $m$ as in Proposition 13. Every partial of every law in $(K,G,N)$
is nonnegative and the exponent on $N$ is strictly positive because
$m<1$, so the transition is order-preserving and each of
$\kappa,\varpi,\iota$ enters it monotonically upward; from a common
start, raising any one of them raises $(K_t,G_t,N_t)$ componentwise at
every $t\ge1$. Since $\sigma>1$ makes $V_W$ strictly increasing in each
$N_t$, the three functionals are strictly positive. $\blacksquare$

The second half of that proof is a standard monotone comparative-statics
argument: the transition is order-preserving and the objective increases
in the state. What is particular to this model is that three
coefficients exhaust the worker’s reach, so the comparative static
yields Theorem 4’s exact algebraic margin rather than a sign alone.

The monotone step is a *worker* statement and does not generalise. The
capitalist’s rate lowers his own consumption while raising the stock it
funds, so his margin is a genuine trade-off and no order-preserving
argument reaches it. His side is left to Theorem 7.

**Corollary 3 (what start-dependence now costs).** *On an interval of
splits proper from the declared start,*
$$\phi^{2}\,\frac{\mathrm{d}\Psi^{\rm fix}}{\mathrm{d}\phi}
  \;=\; -(1-\theta)\,\Omega^{\rm fix}\big(1-\varepsilon_{\rm fix}\big)
        \;-\; \frac{\theta\tau^{2}\alpha^{2}\phi^{2}}{\kappa_W(\phi)^{2}},
\qquad \varepsilon_{\rm fix} \;=\; \frac{\phi\,(\Omega^{\rm fix})'}{\Omega^{\rm fix}} ,$$
*which is Theorem 5’s display term for term, with the loop gain
$\varepsilon_\Omega\varepsilon_g$ replaced by $\varepsilon_{\rm fix}$.
So $\varepsilon_{\rm fix}\le1$ on that interval is sufficient for every
crossing to be downward and hence for single-peakedness of the
fixed-start payoff in the split; the sharp condition is the weaker one
that $\mathrm{d}\Psi^{\rm fix}/\mathrm{d}\phi<0$ at every zero of
$\Psi^{\rm fix}$. Neither is proved here. Equivalently, on any interval
of splits **interior to the box** — $\phi>0$, which the elasticity
already presumes and which excludes the $\phi=0$ corner of Proposition 2
— on which $\Omega^{\rm fix}>0$, the condition
$\varepsilon_{\rm fix}\le1$ is exactly the statement that
$\phi\mapsto\Omega^{\rm fix}(\phi)/\phi$ is non-increasing there: the
two differ by the strictly positive factor $\phi^{2}/\Omega^{\rm fix}$.
The restatement needs no closed form for $\Omega^{\rm fix}$, and it is
what makes the target a monotonicity statement rather than a bound.
Sharper, and by cancellation rather than by estimate: since
$\varpi=\phi\tau$ and
$\mathcal{A}_\varpi=\varpi\,\partial F/\partial\varpi$, the $1/\phi$
divides out exactly, leaving*
$$\frac{\Omega^{\rm fix}(\phi)}{\phi} \;=\; \frac{\theta\tau}{1-\theta}\,
  \frac{\partial F/\partial\varpi}{\partial F/\partial\ln\kappa} ,$$ *so
that $\varepsilon_{\rm fix}\le1$ is exactly the statement that the ratio
of those two partials of the single functional $F$ is non-increasing in
the split. No free-standing $\phi$ survives, and the condition is of the
monotone-ratio type the constrained rule already turns on elsewhere.
Differentiating that ratio is then a finite step rather than a
functional one, because both of its arguments move with the split along
slopes already in hand: $\varpi=\phi\tau$ gives
$\partial\varpi/\partial\phi=\tau$;
$\kappa_W=(1-\alpha)+(1-\phi)\tau\alpha$ gives the
$\partial\ln\kappa/\partial\phi=-\tau\alpha/\kappa_W$ used above; and
the third argument $\iota=i(1-\tau)$ does not move with the split at
all, so the state travels in a fixed direction. Let $\nabla F$ and
$\nabla^{2}F$ be the gradient and Hessian of $F$ in those two moving
arguments $(\ln\kappa,\varpi)$ — which asks $F$ to be twice continuously
differentiable in them along the path, a second-order hypothesis
Proposition 14 does not supply, since it bounds per-date share
elasticities and nothing above them, and which this corollary therefore
adds. Then*
$$\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\phi}\,\frac{\partial F/\partial\varpi}{\mathcal{A}_\kappa}
  \;=\; \frac{\tau}{\mathcal{A}_\kappa^{2}}\,\det\big[\,\nabla F \;\;\; \nabla^{2}F\,\mathsf{v}\,\big],
  \qquad \mathsf{v} \;=\; \Big(-\frac{\alpha}{\kappa_W},\;1\Big)^{\top} ,$$
*with $\tau\mathsf{v}$ the direction of travel. On the stated interval
$\Omega^{\rm fix}>0$ requires $\mathcal{A}_\varpi>0$, and
$\mathcal{A}_\varpi$ is a derivative in $\ln\varpi$, so $\varpi>0$ and
hence $\tau>0$ and $\phi>0$ together; $\mathcal{A}_\kappa>0$ then makes
the prefactor $\tau/\mathcal{A}_\kappa^{2}$ strictly positive, and
$\kappa_W>0$ keeps $\mathsf{v}$ finite. So $\varepsilon_{\rm fix}\le1$
there is exactly the statement that the determinant is non-positive.
That is a third restatement, and like the first two an equivalence
rather than a sufficient condition: nothing has been given up, and
nothing has been proved.*

The parallel with Theorem 5 is exact in the form and does not extend to
the proof, and the distinction is the whole of what makes Corollary 3
hard. Theorem 5 proved a loop gain below one because $\Omega$ has the
closed form (6), which is what let (22) be differentiated and (23)
assembled: the pole making the wedge sensitive to growth is the pole
making growth insensitive to the split, and both poles are visible
because both objects are explicit. $\Omega^{\rm fix}$ is a ratio of two
path functionals and has no closed form off the rest point. The natural
repair — bracketing $\Omega^{\rm fix}(\phi)$ between the two closed-form
endpoints $\Omega(g(\hat\phi))$ and $\Omega(g(\phi))$, and inheriting
Theorem 5’s bound — is refuted at every resolvable split tested
(Computation 10). The obstruction is structural rather than
computational: Theorem 5’s technique does not carry across, and nothing
here suggests a finer grid would make it. What that refutation does not
touch is the monotonicity form above. The bracketing route bounds the
*level* of $\Omega^{\rm fix}$ in order to inherit an elasticity bound,
which is strictly more than $\varepsilon_{\rm fix}\le1$ asks; a proof
that $\Omega^{\rm fix}/\phi$ is non-increasing would need no bracket,
and that route is open.

That open route now has a shape, and the shape says where a proof would
have to come from. Expanding the determinant, and indexing $\nabla^{2}F$
by $1$ for $\ln\kappa$ and $2$ for $\varpi$,
$$\det\big[\,\nabla F \;\;\; \nabla^{2}F\,\mathsf{v}\,\big] \;=\;
  \mathcal{A}_\kappa\,(\nabla^{2}F)_{22}
  \;+\; \frac{\alpha}{\kappa_W}\,\frac{\partial F}{\partial\varpi}\,(\nabla^{2}F)_{11}
  \;-\; (\nabla^{2}F)_{12}\Big[\frac{\partial F}{\partial\varpi}
        +\frac{\alpha}{\kappa_W}\,\mathcal{A}_\kappa\Big] ,$$ every
entry of which is a second derivative of one functional in the two
arguments the split moves: what has to be signed is the curvature of
$F$, not the relative level of two path functionals. And because
$\nabla F$ lies in the open first quadrant — $\mathcal{A}_\kappa>0$ and
$\partial F/\partial\varpi=\mathcal{A}_\varpi/\varpi>0$ — the condition
also reads as a rotation: the gradient must not turn towards the
$\varpi$ axis as the split rises.

At the zeros themselves — the only points the sharp condition
interrogates — the determinant does not have to be signed, because it
collapses. A zero of $\Psi^{\rm fix}$ says $\mathsf{v}\cdot\nabla F=0$:
the gradient is orthogonal to the direction of travel, so
$\nabla F=\mathcal{A}_\kappa\,(1,\ \alpha/\kappa_W)^{\top}$ there, and
the determinant reduces exactly to
$\mathcal{A}_\kappa\,\mathsf{v}^{\top}\nabla^{2}F\,\mathsf{v}$.
Differentiating $V_W(\phi)=F\big(\ln\kappa_W(\phi),\,\phi\tau\big)$
twice at such a zero, $$\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}V_W}{\mathrm{d}\phi^{2}}
  \;=\; \tau^{2}\Big[\,\mathsf{v}^{\top}\nabla^{2}F\,\mathsf{v}
        \;-\;\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\kappa_W^{2}}\,\mathcal{A}_\kappa\Big],$$
the second term being the curvature of $\ln\kappa_W$ in the split alone.
So each crossing asks one scalar question — whether the curvature of $F$
along the single direction $\mathsf{v}$ stays below the strictly
positive threshold $(\alpha^{2}/\kappa_W^{2})\mathcal{A}_\kappa$ — and
the condition carries slack: $F$ need not be concave there, or anywhere.
Per date the quadratic form splits over the weights
$w_t=\beta^{t}N_{W,t}^{1-\sigma}$ into
$\sum_t w_t\,\mathsf{v}^{\top}\big(\nabla^{2}\ln N_{W,t}\big)\mathsf{v}
-(\sigma-1)\sum_t w_t\big(\nabla\ln N_{W,t}\cdot\mathsf{v}\big)^{2}$ —
inherited per-date curvature against an aggregator term that only helps
— and the per-date deviations $\nabla\ln N_{W,t}\cdot\mathsf{v}$ sum to
zero under those weights at the zero, which is the first-order condition
read date by date. Measured at a base-family cell — $\theta=0.80$,
$(\tau,i)=(0.34,0.41)$, incumbent split $0.858$ — the proper range
carries exactly one zero, at $\phi=0.8584$; the collapse identity holds
there to $5\times10^{-14}$ where an off-zero control misses it by
$3\times10^{-2}$; and the crossing is downward with a wide margin, the
direction curvature at $-129.4$ against a threshold of $+21.6$. At the
counterexample cell of Computation 9, from the corner incumbent’s own
rest point, the margin $\mathsf{v}\cdot\nabla F$ is strictly positive at
every proper split scanned: no interior zero exists to interrogate, and
the fixed-start payoff rises monotonically to the corner. One cell each,
and nothing is claimed beyond them. What the collapse changes is the
target: a proof of single-peakedness no longer has to sign a determinant
on an interval, only to bound one direction’s curvature at the crossings
it can locate.

A sufficient condition is immediate, and it is the one thing this route
cannot have. Were $F$ concave in $(\ln\kappa,\varpi)$ and supermodular
in that pair, the first two terms would be non-positive and so would the
third, and $\varepsilon_{\rm fix}\le1$ would hold wherever the
corollary’s remaining hypotheses hold rather than on an interval one has
to certify. But that is the open item wearing a second coat, and not a
weaker condition won by a change of variable: $\kappa_W$ is affine and
positive in the split and $\varpi$ is affine in it, so a concave $F$
increasing in each argument makes $V_W$ concave in the split outright —
Theorem 7’s conclusion for the worker, reached without Theorem 7’s
per-date hypothesis. What this paper reports of the concavity Theorem 7
does ask for — concavity of cumulated log growth in the deviating share
— is that it is checkable, that it holds at the cells computed here, and
that it is false in general. The mechanism is the durability of the
stocks the worker funds. Where both depreciate fully the stock-law
numerators lose their survival terms, every date’s log growth is a
nonnegative combination of the logarithms of three affine coefficients,
and the payoff is strictly concave outright; restore the survival terms
and the same logarithm becomes a log-sum-exp, convex exactly where the
argument needs it affine.

What the determinant does buy is the shape of a failure, and it is less
of a screen than it first looks. Assume only that the two own second
derivatives are non-positive — weaker than concavity, which constrains
the cross term as well. The first two terms are then non-positive, and
the third is $-(\nabla^{2}F)_{12}$ times a strictly positive number, so
a positive determinant requires $(\nabla^{2}F)_{12}<0$. That condition
is necessary and it is weak, and it is not a statement about
substitutability between the two coefficients. Because the period payoff
is $N_{W,t}^{1-\sigma}$ with $\sigma>1$, the cross partial carries the
term
$$(1-\sigma)\sum_{t}w_t\,\frac{\partial\ln N_{W,t}}{\partial\ln\kappa}\,
   \frac{\partial\ln N_{W,t}}{\partial\varpi},
 \qquad w_t=\beta^{t}N_{W,t}^{1-\sigma}>0 ,$$ at the same weights the
proof of Proposition 14 uses, and that term is negative whenever the two
elasticity paths are non-negative and strictly positive at a common date
— non-negativity is what the monotone step of that proof supplies, and
the public stock’s one-period feed-through delay is why the common date,
rather than mere non-vanishing, is the thing to ask for. It stays
negative when the cumulated log growth is additively separable in the
two arguments, that is, where the technology carries no interaction
between them at all: there the sign is supplied by the curvature of the
aggregator and not by the primitives. Two things follow. Supermodularity
of $F$ is not a mild strengthening of concavity but a requirement held
against that term, so neither leg of the sufficient condition comes free
— the concavity leg is the one measured false in general, and the
supermodularity leg would have to be won against the aggregator’s own
curvature rather than read off the technology. And what must be large is
the cross term relative to the two own terms rather than in itself: in
the narrower benchmark where the cumulated log growth is affine in both
arguments, that comparison is one between the discounted covariance of
the two elasticity paths and their variances, and it vanishes whenever
the two paths are proportional. None of Computation 10’s $268$ points
exhibits a failure, which establishes nothing — those points cannot
establish $\varepsilon_{\rm fix}\le1$ either — but its elasticity test
is not swept in $\alpha$, $\gamma$, $\sigma$ or $\beta$, so it is not
the search that would settle the question.

What that refutation buys is the mechanism, measured rather than argued.
$\Omega^{\rm fix}$ *rises* in the split while the diagonal $\Omega$
*falls*, the two crossing at $\hat\phi$ where Proposition 14 forces them
to agree. Where that crossing is witnessed $\varepsilon_{\rm fix}>0$,
and above $\hat\phi$ the fixed-start wedge sits above the diagonal one —
precisely the direction that opposes the downward crossing. The sign is
not structural and nothing below asks it to be: Computation 10 puts
$\varepsilon_{\rm fix}$ in $[-0.138,+0.213]$ over its $268$ points, and
Corollary 3 needs only $\varepsilon_{\rm fix}\le1$. That is the same
thing the start-dependence says in words: from the incumbent’s rest
point a candidate above $\hat\phi$ is evaluated at a public stock lower
than its own rest point would carry, which raises the marginal value of
the split, and below $\hat\phi$ the argument reverses. Single-peakedness
is a race, as Theorem 5’s was, and that is why a bound is needed where a
sign would not do.

**Computation 10 (the factorisation, and the elasticity that is left).**
*At nine cells over $48$ splits each, the chain-rule identity (34)
reproduces a direct central difference of $V_W$ in $\phi$ to a maximum
relative error of $2.7\times10^{-5}$, with a Richardson ratio of $4.00$
at eight of the nine — truncation, not disagreement. The same three
functionals reproduce the **tax** margin to $1.2\times10^{-7}$, which a
wrong parameterisation could not do. The three functionals
$\mathcal{A}_\kappa$, $\mathcal{A}_\varpi$ and $\mathcal{A}_\iota$ are
positive at every point tested, and $\Omega^{\rm fix}=\Omega$ at a start
equal to the induced rest point to $4.6\times10^{-7}$, an error that
falls from $3.2\times10^{-2}$ to $6.7\times10^{-10}$ as the horizon runs
$800\to12800$ at the slowest-converging cell. Over $268$ points
$\varepsilon_{\rm fix}$ lies in $[-0.138,+0.213]$, against the threshold
of $1$, and $\mathrm{d}\Psi^{\rm fix}/\mathrm{d}\phi<0$ at the zero in
$5$ of $5$ discriminating cells. Bracketing $\Omega^{\rm fix}$ between
$\Omega(g(\hat\phi))$ and $\Omega(g(\phi))$ fails at $13$ of $13$
resolvable splits, which is what rules Theorem 5’s technique out rather
than merely leaving it unused. Over $\phi\in[0.26,0.98]$ at the base
cell $\Omega^{\rm fix}$ rises from $0.490$ to $0.574$ while $\Omega$
falls from $0.594$ to $0.560$, the two crossing at $\hat\phi$.*

*What it does not cover is reported rather than netted out. A truncation
gate drops $135$ of $432$ splits, and two whole cells — $\theta=0.95$
and $\delta_G=0.20$ — contribute nothing to the wedge and elasticity
tests; at $\delta_G=0.20$ a further $29$ of $48$ splits are improper.
Near-improper profiles are exactly where a finite sum cannot represent
the object, and they are the region this computation is silent about.
All six cells with a crossing share $\delta_G=0.05$, $\alpha=0.33$,
$\gamma=0.5$, $\sigma=2$, $\beta=0.96$, $\delta=0.08$: the elasticity
was not swept in $\alpha$, $\gamma$, $\sigma$ or $\beta$. Two hundred
and sixty-eight points can refute $\varepsilon_{\rm fix}\le1$ and did
not; they cannot establish it.*

So the position is this, and it differs by instrument. The two routes
are complementary rather than alternative: Theorem 7 asks for curvature
at every date and covers either class, and Proposition 14 asks for no
curvature anywhere and covers the worker only.

For the **worker’s split**, Proposition 14 is the stronger statement.
The fixed-start margin keeps Theorem 4’s algebraic form unconditionally,
with a path functional in place of the wedge, so what separates a
stationary split from Definition 1 is no longer a translation but a
single elasticity — and that elasticity sits in the same relation to
Corollary 3 as the loop gain of Theorem 5 sits to Corollary 2. The gap
is **reduced, not closed**: $\varepsilon_{\rm fix}\le1$ is measured at
nine cells with a wide margin and proved nowhere.

For the **capitalist’s rate**, only Theorem 7 reaches. Proposition 14’s
monotone step is a worker statement by construction, because the rate
lowers his consumption while raising the stock it funds. So his side is
closed exactly where cumulated concavity holds, and that hypothesis —
Appendix C.8’s observation, promoted here to a theorem with its two free
dates, the recursion that says which date binds, and the closed form
(31)–(32) that turns the tail into one inequality between quantities the
paper already carries — holds with room at the cells reported here and
does not hold generally. The term that breaks it is the one full
depreciation would delete: durable stocks make $\mathcal{G}$ concave in
$\ln g$, and a concave $\mathcal{G}$ feeds curvature back through the
inverse with the wrong sign. The counterexample is exact and carries a
discount weight of $10^{-21}$, which is a different object from an open
gap and a weaker one than a closed clause.

## 8.7 The pair, in the coordinates its instruments act through

As those statements stand, the joint condition in $(\tau,\phi)$ is
reached by neither. Proposition 14 delivers the tax margin in the same
three functionals — its second display — without delivering the
curvature of the pair; and Proposition 13(v) appears to close the other
route for every $\alpha\le\frac12$. The apparent obstruction is
coordinate-dependent: the product that creates the saddle in
$(\tau,\phi)$ becomes the relevant coordinate in $(\tau,\varpi)$.

Concavity is a property of a *parameterisation*. Being a global
maximiser is not: it survives any bijection of the feasible set, because
a bijection preserves the set of attained values. Definition 1 asks only
the second question — whether the incumbent can be beaten — so a saddle
found in one coordinate system is a fact about that coordinate system
and licenses nothing about the route until the coordinates have been
chosen well. Proposition 13(v)’s algebra is correct and is used below
unchanged. What does not follow from it is its final clause.

The coordinates choose themselves, and Proposition 14 has already named
them. The three coefficients (33) are the whole of the worker’s reach,
and the second of them is the very product $\varpi=\phi\tau$ whose
bilinearity makes $\kappa_W$ a saddle in $(\tau,\phi)$. Take
$(\tau,\varpi)$ as the choice variables. Then
$$\kappa \;=\; (1-\alpha)+\alpha(\tau-\varpi), \qquad \varpi \;=\; \varpi,
  \qquad \iota \;=\; i(1-\tau) \tag{35}$$ are all three **affine**, and
the instrument box becomes the triangle
$T=\{(\tau,\varpi): 0<\varpi\le\tau\le\bar\tau\}$, which is **convex**.
Everything the worker’s date-wise recursion is built from is then a
logarithm of a positive affine function, and one standard fact covers
all of it at once.

**Lemma 2 (log-affine concavity).** *Let $L_1,\dots,L_n$ be affine and
strictly positive on a convex $S\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}$ and let
$\lambda_1,\dots,\lambda_n\ge0$. Then $f=\sum_k\lambda_k\ln L_k$ is
concave on $S$, with*
$$-\nabla^{2}f \;=\; \sum_k \lambda_k\,\frac{\nabla L_k\,\nabla L_k^{\top}}{L_k^{2}}
  \;\succeq\; 0 , \tag{36}$$ *a sum of rank-one positive semidefinite
terms; and $f$ is strictly concave as soon as the gradients $\nabla L_k$
carrying $\lambda_k>0$ span $\mathbb{R}^{d}$.*

*Proof.* $\nabla^{2}\ln L = -\nabla L\,\nabla L^{\top}/L^{2}$ for affine
$L$, since $\nabla^{2}L=0$; sum with nonnegative weights. The quadratic
form at $v$ is $-\sum_k\lambda_k(\nabla L_k\cdot
v)^{2}/L_k^{2}$, which is strictly negative for every $v\neq0$ exactly
when no $v$ is orthogonal to every $\nabla L_k$ with $\lambda_k>0$.
$\blacksquare$

The lemma is standard and is claimed here as an application, not as a
technique. What is particular to this model is (35): that the change of
variable putting the worker’s instruments into it is the one Proposition
14 was already using for a different purpose.

**Proposition 15 (the pair, in the product coordinates).** *Let
Assumptions 1–3 hold with $\theta\in(0,1)$, fix a start with $x_0,z_0>0$
and a capitalist’s rate $i>0$ — parts (ii)–(iii) need them, the
gradients that span $\mathbb{R}^{2}$ there carrying $x_0^{\alpha}$ and
$i$ — and let the worker’s pair range over $[0,\bar\tau]\times[0,1]$.
Part (i) uses admissibility (Assumption 5) to discard the collapsed edge
and nothing else; parts (iv)–(v) are statements at an interior rest
point and carry Proposition 6’s hypotheses, since they differentiate
$g$. Then:*

*(i) **The reparameterisation is a bijection onto a convex set.**
$(\tau,\phi)\mapsto(\tau,\phi\tau)$ is a bijection of $\{\tau>0\}$ onto
$\{0\le\varpi\le\tau\le\bar\tau,\ \tau>0\}$, a diffeomorphism on the
interior with Jacobian determinant $\tau>0$, and it carries global
maximisers to global maximisers. On the edges it collapses or flattens,
$\tau=0$ and $\varpi=0$, the public stock has no source term and decays
exactly, $G_t=(1-\delta_G)^{t}G_0$, and the edge splits in two rather
than being disposed of at once. At $\delta_G=1$, $z_1=0$, so $g_{W,1}=0$
and the payoff is $-\infty$ at date two. At $\delta_G<1$ either the
worker’s dynasty shrinks more slowly than $(1-\delta_G)^{t}$, in which
case $z_t\to0$ and $\theta<1$ drives $g_{W,t}\to0$, or it does not, in
which case its growth factor is eventually at or below $1-\delta_G$ and
properness requires $(1-\delta_G)^{\sigma-1}>\beta$. **So where
$(1-\delta_G)^{\sigma-1}\le\beta$ the edge is inadmissible-improper and
Assumption 5 disposes of it; where that inequality runs the other way it
need not be, and admissibility alone does not exclude it** — what does
is that a dynasty growing at $1-\delta_G$ or less is beaten by an
incumbent whose own criterion (A) is $g>1-\delta_G$, which is a
comparison and not a hypothesis. Nothing below rests on which case
holds: $T$ is convex whether or not its $\varpi=0$ face is attached, and
Lemma 2 needs the face detached in either case. The worker’s problem is
therefore the maximisation of
$\widetilde V_W(\tau,\varpi)=V_W(\tau,\varpi/\tau)$ over the convex set
$T$.*

*(ii) **Proposition 13(v)’s saddle is a property of the coordinates.**
On $T$, $\ln\kappa$ is concave, its Hessian being rank one and negative
semidefinite by Lemma 2 with $\nabla\kappa=(\alpha,-\alpha)$.
Proposition 13(v)’s determinant is unchanged and so is its sign; what
changes is that it is no longer the determinant the question turns on.*

*(iii) **Two dates are free jointly.** $\ell_0$ and $\ell_1$ are concave
on $T$ under no condition beyond the assumptions, $\ell_1$ strictly.
Explicitly $\ell_0=\mathrm{const}+\gamma\theta\ln\kappa$ and*
$$\ell_1 \;=\; \mathrm{const} \;+\; \gamma\theta(1-m)\ln\kappa \;+\; \gamma\theta\alpha\ln P_0
  \;+\; \gamma(1-\theta)\ln R_0 , \tag{37}$$ *with
$P_0=(1-\delta)x_0+\iota\alpha Ax_0^{\alpha}$ and
$R_0=(1-\delta_G)z_0+\varpi\alpha
Ax_0^{\alpha}$ affine in $\tau$ and in $\varpi$ respectively, and all
three weights strictly positive because $m<1$. This is Proposition 13(i)
for the pair, and it costs the same nothing.*

*(iv) **$\mathcal{P}$ is strictly concave on $T$.** Writing
$h_\kappa,h_\tau,h_\varpi$ for the three entries of
$-\nabla^{2}\mathcal{P}$ — $h_\kappa=\gamma\theta\alpha^{2}/\kappa^{2}$,
$h_\tau=\gamma\alpha/[(1-\alpha)(1-\tau)^{2}]$ and
$h_\varpi=\gamma(1-\theta)/\varpi^{2}$ —* $$-\nabla^{2}\mathcal{P} \;=\;
  \begin{pmatrix} h_\kappa+h_\tau & -h_\kappa \\ -h_\kappa & h_\kappa+h_\varpi \end{pmatrix},
\qquad \det\big(-\nabla^{2}\mathcal{P}\big) \;=\; h_\kappa(h_\tau+h_\varpi)+h_\tau h_\varpi \;>\; 0 . \tag{38}$$

*(v) **The tail is one scalar inequality again.** At an interior rest
point $\ell_u=\ln g$ at every date, so joint concavity of
$(\tau,\varpi)\mapsto\ln g$ on $T$ is necessary for the hypothesis.
Differentiating $\mathcal{G}(\ln g)=\mathcal{P}$ twice gives*
$$\nabla^{2}\ln g \;=\; \frac{1}{\mathcal{G}'}\Big[\nabla^{2}\mathcal{P}
   \;-\; \frac{\mathcal{G}''}{(\mathcal{G}')^{2}}\,\nabla\mathcal{P}\,\nabla\mathcal{P}^{\top}\Big],$$
*and since $\mathcal{G}''\le0$ makes the rank-one correction positive
semidefinite while (iv) makes $-\nabla^{2}\mathcal{P}$ positive
definite, $\ln g$ is concave on $T$ if and only if*
$$\frac{|\mathcal{G}''|}{(\mathcal{G}')^{2}}\;
  \nabla\mathcal{P}^{\top}\big(-\nabla^{2}\mathcal{P}\big)^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{P} \;\le\; 1 .
  \tag{$\ast\ast$}$$ *Restricted to the split direction $(\ast\ast)$ is
exactly $(\ast)$; and because $p^{\top}\!A^{-1}p\ge p_2^{2}/A_{22}$ for
positive definite $A$, $(\ast\ast)$ implies $(\ast)$ and is not implied
by it. The joint criterion is strictly the harder one, as joint
concavity must be.*

*Proof.* **(i)** $\phi=\varpi/\tau$ inverts the map where $\tau>0$, and
the Jacobian
$\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\ \phi&\tau\end{smallmatrix}\right)$ has
determinant $\tau>0$. A bijection of the feasible set preserves the
attained value set, hence carries maximisers to maximisers. For the
excluded edges, $\varpi=0$ leaves the public law with no source, so
$G_t=(1-\delta_G)^{t}G_0$ exactly and $z_t=(1-\delta_G)^{t}G_0/N_{W,t}$.
At $\delta_G=1$ that is $z_1=0$, so $g_{W,1}=0$ and $N_{W,2}=0$. At
$\delta_G<1$ the two cases are exhaustive by construction. If
$N_{W,t}(1-\delta_G)^{-t}\to\infty$ then $z_t\to0$, and Assumption 2
with $\theta<1$ sends $g_{W,t}\to0$, so $\beta g_W^{1-\sigma}$ diverges.
Otherwise $N_{W,t}$ is bounded by a multiple of $(1-\delta_G)^{t}$, so
the geometric mean of $g_W$ is at most $1-\delta_G$ and Assumption 5
needs $(1-\delta_G)^{\sigma-1}>\beta$. Where that fails, the edge is
improper; where it holds, this argument does not make it improper, and
none below asks it to — Proposition 2(iii) already rules out
improperness as a universal. **(ii)–(iv)** are Lemma 2 applied to
$\ln\kappa$; to the three terms of (37), whose gradients
$(\alpha,-\alpha)$, $(-i\alpha Ax_0^{\alpha},0)$ and
$(0,\alpha Ax_0^{\alpha})$ span $\mathbb{R}^{2}$; and to the three
logarithms of $\mathcal{P}$, whose gradients $(\alpha,-\alpha)$, $(1,0)$
and $(0,1)$ span $\mathbb{R}^{2}$. Equation (37) is Proposition 13(i)’s
derivation with one difference: $P_0$ now carries $\tau$, through
$\iota$, and carries it affinely — so the extra channel the joint
problem opens is itself concave rather than an obstruction. **(v)**
Differentiating $\mathcal{G}(\ln g)=\mathcal{P}$ twice gives
$\mathcal{G}'\nabla^{2}\ln g+\mathcal{G}''\nabla\ln g\,\nabla\ln g^{\top}=\nabla^{2}\mathcal{P}$
with $\nabla\ln g=\nabla\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{G}'$, which rearranges as
displayed; (31)–(32) supply $\mathcal{G}'>0$ and $\mathcal{G}''\le0$.
Writing $A=-\nabla^{2}\mathcal{P}\succ0$, $p=\nabla\mathcal{P}$ and
$\rho=|\mathcal{G}''|/(\mathcal{G}')^{2}\ge0$, the requirement
$\nabla^{2}\ln g\preceq0$ is $A-\rho pp^{\top}\succeq0$; the matrix
determinant lemma gives
$\det(A-\rho pp^{\top})=(1-\rho\,p^{\top}\!A^{-1}p)\det A$, and a
rank-one downdate of a positive definite matrix is positive semidefinite
exactly when that factor is nonnegative, which is $(\ast\ast)$. The
restriction is the $(2,2)$ entry, and
$p^{\top}\!A^{-1}p-p_2^{2}/A_{22}=(A_{22}p_1-A_{12}p_2)^{2}/(A_{22}\det A)\ge0$.
$\blacksquare$

Condition $(\ast\ast)$ is a statement about a point of $T$ at a growth
factor that point induces, so reading it off a computation says nothing
about a neighbouring economy. It has a sufficient condition in the
primitives that is free of the entire dynamic block, and stating it is
worth a proposition because of what it does *not* contain.

**Proposition 15$'$ (a primitive sufficient condition for $(\ast\ast)$,
free of $\gamma$, of both depreciation rates and of the growth
factor).** *Let Proposition 15’s hypotheses hold and write
$b_1=\gamma\alpha/(1-\alpha)$, $b_2=\gamma(1-\theta)$ and*
$$\Phi_c(\alpha,\theta) \;=\; \frac{1-\alpha}{1-\min\big(\alpha,\;(1-\theta)(1-\alpha)\big)},
\qquad
\Phi(\tau,\varpi) \;=\; \frac{\kappa_W^{2}}{\theta} \;+\; \frac{\alpha^{2}\varpi^{2}}{1-\theta}
  \;+\; \alpha(1-\alpha)(1-\tau)^{2} ,$$ *with
$\kappa_W=(1-\alpha)+\alpha(\tau-\varpi)$ and $\varpi=\phi\tau$. This
$\Phi$ is the weighted sum the proof below assembles from Lemma 2; it is
**not** the reproduction composite of $(4)$, which carries the same
glyph in a different section. If
$\Phi(\tau,\varpi)\le\Phi_c(\alpha,\theta)$ then $(\ast\ast)$ holds
strictly. The hypothesis reads only $(\alpha,\theta,\tau,\phi)$: neither
$\gamma$ nor $\delta$, $\delta_G$, $\beta$, $\sigma$, $i$, $A$, $B$ nor
$g$ appears in it.*

*Proof.* Two steps, and the second is where the parameters cancel.

**Step 1:
$\rho\equiv|\mathcal{G}''|/(\mathcal{G}')^{2}<1/\min(b_1,b_2)$, with no
condition beyond Assumption 4.** This $\rho$ is the curvature ratio the
step below assembles; it is **not** the capitalist’s valuation of the
workforce $a_{N_W}/\varphi$ of §2.4 and (12), which carries the same
glyph in a different section. Differentiating $\mathcal{H}$ of
Proposition 6(ii) twice and writing $\mu_K=g/(g-1+\delta)$,
$\mu=g/(g-1+\delta_G)$ as in (21),
$$\mathcal{G}' = 1+b_1\mu_K+b_2\mu , \qquad
  |\mathcal{G}''| = b_1\mu_K(\mu_K-1)+b_2\mu(\mu-1)\;\ge\;0 ,$$ the sign
being Assumption 4’s, since (A) gives $\mu_K,\mu\ge1$. Put $a=b_1\mu_K$,
$c=b_2\mu$, so that $\mathcal{G}'=1+a+c$ and
$|\mathcal{G}''|=a^{2}/b_1-a+c^{2}/b_2-c$. Then for any $m>0$,
$$(\mathcal{G}')^{2}-m|\mathcal{G}''| \;=\; 1+2a+2c+2ac+m(a+c)
   +a^{2}\Big(1-\frac{m}{b_1}\Big)+c^{2}\Big(1-\frac{m}{b_2}\Big) ,$$ an
identity in $(a,c,b_1,b_2,m)$. At $m=\min(b_1,b_2)$ the last two
brackets are nonnegative and every other term is strictly positive, so
the whole exceeds $1$; dividing by $(\mathcal{G}')^{2}>0$ gives the
claim. Nothing here uses $\gamma$, the level of $g$, or how $\mu_K$ and
$\mu$ were reached.

**Step 2: $p^{\top}\!A^{-1}p$ has a closed form, and dividing by
$\min(b_1,b_2)$ cancels $\gamma$.** By Lemma 2 the matrix and the vector
of $(\ast\ast)$ are the same weighted family over the three log-affine
coefficients of $\mathcal{P}$: $A=\sum_k\lambda_kw_kw_k^{\top}$ and
$p=\sum_k\lambda_kw_k$ with $w_k=\nabla L_k/L_k$,
$\lambda=(\gamma\theta,\,b_2,\,b_1)$ and
$L=(\kappa_W,\,\varpi,\,1-\tau)$. For such a pair, with $c$ spanning the
one-dimensional kernel of $c\mapsto\sum_kc_kw_k$,
$$p^{\top}\!A^{-1}p \;=\; \sum_k\lambda_k-\frac{\big(\sum_kc_k\big)^{2}}{\sum_kc_k^{2}/\lambda_k} ,
\qquad c=\Big(\tfrac{\kappa_W}{\alpha},\ \varpi,\ 1-\tau\Big) .$$ Both
pieces collapse.
$\sum_k\lambda_k=\gamma\theta+\gamma(1-\theta)+b_1=\gamma/(1-\alpha)$,
and since $\kappa_W=(1-\alpha)+\alpha(\tau-\varpi)$,
$$\sum_kc_k=\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}+\tau-\varpi+\varpi+(1-\tau)=\frac{1}{\alpha}$$
identically — free of $\tau$, $\varpi$, $\theta$ and $\gamma$ alike —
while $\sum_kc_k^{2}/\lambda_k=\Phi/(\gamma\alpha^{2})$ with $\Phi$ as
in the statement above. Hence
$$p^{\top}\!A^{-1}p \;=\; \gamma\Big[\frac{1}{1-\alpha}-\frac{1}{\Phi}\Big] \qquad\text{exactly.}$$
Combining, $(\ast\ast)$’s left-hand side is below
$\gamma\big[\tfrac{1}{1-\alpha}-\tfrac{1}{\Phi}\big]/\min(b_1,b_2)$, and
$\min(b_1,b_2)
=\gamma\min\big(\tfrac{\alpha}{1-\alpha},1-\theta\big)$ carries the same
factor $\gamma$, which therefore cancels. The bound is at most one
exactly when
$\tfrac{1}{1-\alpha}-\tfrac{1}{\Phi}\le\min\big(\tfrac{\alpha}{1-\alpha},1-\theta\big)$,
and solving for $\Phi$ gives the stated bound. $\blacksquare$

Two things follow that the pointwise statement does not give. The
condition is a **sublevel set of a strictly convex quadratic** —
$\Phi$’s Hessian in $(\tau,\varpi)$ is constant with positive diagonal
and positive determinant — so $\{\Phi\le\Phi_c\}\cap T$ is convex, an
ellipse cut by the triangle, rather than a collection of cells. And
because the hypothesis omits $\gamma$, $\delta$, $\delta_G$ and $g$, no
condition in those coordinates alone can decide $(\ast\ast)$: a sweep
that varies them and reports where the criterion breaks is describing
the region it happened to visit, not a mechanism. At the reported cells
$\Phi_c=0.9153$ against $\Phi=0.8715$ at $(\tau,\phi)=(0.50,1)$ and
$0.8721$ at $(0.51,1)$, so $(\ast\ast)$ holds there at **every**
admissible $(\gamma,\delta,\delta_G,\beta,\sigma,i,A,B)$ and every $g$
at once — which no finite grid over those coordinates could have
established, however fine. The condition is sufficient and not
necessary: it is silent at $\varpi\to0$, where $\Phi$ is large and
$(\ast\ast)$ may hold or fail.

That silence is not slack in the bound, and saying so takes the argument
that produced $\Phi_c$ and runs it backwards. Fix any
$(\tau,\varpi)\in T$ with $\Phi>\Phi_c$. Since
$R=\gamma\big[1/(1-\alpha)-1/\Phi\big]$ carries
$(\alpha,\gamma,\theta,\tau,\varpi)$ and nothing else, and is strictly
increasing in $\Phi$, that is exactly $R>\min(b_1,b_2)$ — the negation
of Step 1. And $\rho\to1/b_1$ as $g\downarrow1-\delta$ when
$\delta<\delta_G$, with the mirror-image limit $1/b_2$ when
$\delta_G<\delta$ and $1/(b_1+b_2)$ when they are equal, so the supremum
Step 1 bounds is attained in the limit rather than merely approached.
Because $\mathcal{H}$ is strictly increasing (Theorem 2(ii)), $B$ places
$g$ anywhere above its floor, and
$\delta<\min\big(\delta_G,\,1-\beta^{1/(\sigma-1)}\big)$ keeps a whole
right neighbourhood of $1-\delta$ interior and proper. So above
**every** exterior point there are admissible
$(\gamma,\delta,\delta_G,\beta,\sigma,i,A,B)$ at which $(\ast\ast)$
fails, and no larger parameter-free region — of any shape, convex or not
— certifies the criterion. The ellipse is not one certificate among
several: it is the whole of what a condition in $(\tau,\varpi)$ alone
can deliver, which is why the residue below is stated in the other
coordinates rather than in these.

Theorem 7 needs no restatement beyond its domain. Its proof uses
$\sigma>1$, the convexity of the exponential, and the closure of convex
functions under sums and pointwise limits; no step of it refers to the
dimension of the deviating instrument. Read with $I$ any convex subset
of the instrument space — which $T$ is — it stands verbatim, and it then
speaks to the pair.

**Corollary 4 (what the pair now costs).** *Let a stationary profile be
evaluated from the rest point it induces, let $\theta\in(0,1)$, and
suppose $\ln N_{W,t}$ is concave on $T$ for every $t$. Then
$\widetilde V_W$ is concave on $T$ by Theorem 7; the profile satisfies
the first-order condition there, Theorem 4 turning each instrument’s
one-shot condition into $\nabla\widetilde V_W=0$, or, at the corner
$\phi=1$, into the statement that no feasible direction ascends — the
feasible cone on the edge $\varpi=\tau$ being
$\{d: d_\varpi\le d_\tau\}$, on which
$\nabla\widetilde V_W\cdot d=\partial_\varpi\widetilde V_W\,(d_\varpi-d_\tau)\le0$
because $\partial_\tau\widetilde V_W=-\partial_\varpi\widetilde V_W$
there and $\partial_\varpi\widetilde V_W\ge0$ is what puts the split at
its corner. The profile is therefore the worker’s global best reply over
his whole instrument box, and his clause of Definition 1 closes — on
that hypothesis and no other.*

The joint condition is *not* proved. It requires the date-wise
sufficient hypothesis of Theorem 7, with the same two free dates and
alternating recursion. Its tail criterion is $(\ast\ast)$ rather than
$(\ast)$: it is strictly harder, false at the same cell, and false on
about twice as much of the swept grid. The remaining issue is the
intermediate dates, as for the split. The route remains open in the
product coordinates; it was closed only in $(\tau,\phi)$.

There is one face of the assumption set on which the hypothesis costs
nothing, and Proposition 13(iv) has already half-named it. Assumption 1
admits $\delta=\delta_G=1$, and there the tail criterion is vacuous
because $\mathcal{G}''=0$. That is a statement about the limit. The
stronger one is about every date, it holds jointly rather than in the
split alone, and it does not follow from $\mathcal{G}''=0$: recursion
(30) carries inherited curvature forward with its **sign reversed**, so
nonnegative coefficients have to be established and not read off. At
full depreciation they can be written down.

**Proposition 16 (at full depreciation the hypothesis is a theorem).**
*Let Assumptions 1–3 and 5 hold with $\theta\in(0,1)$ and
$\delta=\delta_G=1$, fix a start with $x_0,z_0>0$ and a rate $i>0$, and
let the worker’s pair range over $T$. With
$m=\gamma[\theta\alpha+(1-\theta)]$ and $\chi=\alpha-m$, at every date*
$$\ell_t \;=\; \mathrm{const}_t \;+\; \mathsf{w}^{\kappa}_t\ln\kappa
  \;+\; \mathsf{w}^{\iota}_t\ln\iota \;+\; \mathsf{w}^{\varpi}_t\ln\varpi , \tag{39}$$
*the constants carrying the start and no instrument, and, for $t\ge1$,*
$$\mathsf{w}^{\kappa}_t = \frac{\gamma\theta\big(1-\alpha+m\chi^{t}\big)}{1-\alpha+m}, \quad
  \mathsf{w}^{\iota}_t = \alpha\Big[\gamma\theta
     + \frac{m(1-\gamma\theta)\big(1-\chi^{t-1}\big)}{1-\alpha+m}\Big], \quad
  \mathsf{w}^{\varpi}_t = \frac{\gamma(1-\theta)\big(1-\alpha+m\chi^{t-1}\big)}{1-\alpha+m},
  \tag{40}$$ *while $\mathsf{w}^{\kappa}_0=\gamma\theta$ and
$\mathsf{w}^{\iota}_0=\mathsf{w}^{\varpi}_0=0$. All three weights are
nonnegative at every date and strictly positive from $t=1$. Hence every
$\ell_t$ is concave on $T$, strictly so for $t\ge1$; $\ln N_{W,t}$ is
concave on $T$ at every $t$; and Corollary 4’s hypothesis holds with no
side condition. Moreover the path converges to an interior rest point
from any such start, so Assumption 4 is not a further restriction here;
and the set on which the profile is proper is itself **convex**, so it
is a legitimate domain for Theorem 7, which asks for one.*

*Proof.* One point of bookkeeping first, since the hypothesis list is
shorter than Proposition 13’s. The only thing borrowed from that
proposition is (30), and (30) is Assumption 2’s $\ln g_W$ with $(\star)$
substituted into it: it is an identity along a path, uses no rest point
and no linearisation, and Assumption 4 does not enter it. Assumption 4
enters only through Definition 1 in Corollary 5, and at full
depreciation its condition (A) reads $g>0$, which Assumption 2 gives.

At $\delta=\delta_G=1$ the two stock numerators lose their survival
terms and become monomials in the state,
$\ln P_u=\ln\iota+\ln(\alpha A)+\alpha\ln x_u$ and
$\ln R_u=\ln\varpi+\ln(\alpha A)+\alpha\ln x_u$. The state then reduces
from three logs to two, and this is where $\theta<1$ earns its place
rather than inheriting it: Assumption 2 makes
$\ell_u=\ln B+\gamma\theta\ln(\kappa A)+\gamma\theta\alpha\ln x_u+\gamma(1-\theta)\ln z_u$,
so
$$\ln z_u \;=\; \frac{\ell_u-\ln B-\gamma\theta\ln(\kappa A)-\gamma\theta\alpha\ln x_u}
                     {\gamma(1-\theta)}$$ recovers the third log from
the first two, and the change of variable is a bijection precisely when
$\gamma(1-\theta)\neq0$. At $\theta=1$ the public stock leaves
reproduction, the coefficient is zero and the reduction is unavailable —
which is the no-social-wage corner of Section 4 and not a case this
proposition speaks to. Substituting into (30), and using that constant
shares make the intercept date-invariant,
$$\begin{pmatrix}\ell_{u+1}\\ \ln x_{u+1}\end{pmatrix} =
  \mathcal{R}\begin{pmatrix}\ell_{u}\\ \ln x_{u}\end{pmatrix} + \mathsf{d}, \qquad
  \mathcal{R}=\begin{pmatrix}-m & \alpha m\\ -1 & \alpha\end{pmatrix}
   =\begin{pmatrix}m\\ 1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}-1 & \alpha\end{pmatrix},$$
where $\mathsf{d}$’s first entry is
$\mathrm{const}+\gamma\theta\ln\kappa+\gamma\theta\alpha\ln\iota
+\gamma(1-\theta)\ln\varpi$ and its second is $\mathrm{const}+\ln\iota$.
$\mathcal{R}$ is **rank one**: writing $\mathcal{R}=vw^{\top}$ with
$v=(m,1)^{\top}$ and $w^{\top}=(-1,\alpha)$ gives
$\mathcal{R}^{2}=v(w^{\top}v)w^{\top}=\chi\mathcal{R}$ with the scalar
$\chi=w^{\top}v=\alpha-m$, so $\mathcal{R}^{k}=\chi^{k-1}\mathcal{R}$
for every $k\ge1$ by induction, and
$$\begin{pmatrix}\ell_{t}\\ \ln x_{t}\end{pmatrix}
  = \mathcal{R}^{t}\begin{pmatrix}\ell_{0}\\ \ln x_{0}\end{pmatrix}
    + \big(\mathrm{I}+\Sigma_t\mathcal{R}\big)\mathsf{d} ,
  \qquad \Sigma_t=\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\chi^{k-1}=\frac{1-\chi^{t-1}}{1-\chi},$$
which is (39) with the weights (40) once the entries are collected;
$\ell_0=\mathrm{const}
+\gamma\theta\ln\kappa$ supplies $\mathsf{w}^{\kappa}_0=\gamma\theta$
and $\mathsf{w}^{\iota}_0=\mathsf{w}^{\varpi}_0=0$.

For the signs, $1-\chi=1-\alpha+m>0$, so $\Sigma_t\ge0$ whenever
$\chi^{t-1}\le1$, which $|\chi|<1$ gives; and both remaining questions
reduce to a single inequality, since
$$1-m\Sigma_t=\frac{1-\alpha+m\chi^{t-1}}{1-\alpha+m},
\qquad \frac{\mathsf{w}^{\kappa}_t}{\gamma\theta}=\frac{1-\alpha+m\chi^{t}}{1-\alpha+m} .$$
So it suffices that $1-\alpha+m\chi^{k}>0$ at every integer $k\ge0$. If
$\chi\ge0$ this is immediate. If $\chi<0$ then $m>\alpha$ and
$|\chi|=m-\alpha$, and $m<1$ gives $m(m-\alpha)<m-\alpha<1-\alpha$, so
$1-\alpha+m\chi^{k}\ge1-\alpha-m(m-\alpha)>0$. Then
$\mathsf{w}^{\iota}_t\ge\alpha\gamma\theta>0$ for $t\ge1$. The three
drivers $\kappa=(1-\alpha)+\alpha(\tau-\varpi)$, $\iota=i(1-\tau)$ and
$\varpi$ are affine and strictly positive on the interior of $T$, so
(39) is a nonnegative combination of logarithms of positive affine
functions and Lemma 2 applies; the gradients $(\alpha,-\alpha)$,
$(-i,0)$ and $(0,1)$ span $\mathbb{R}^{2}$ and all three weights are
strictly positive from $t=1$, so the concavity is strict there. A sum of
concave functions is concave.

For the last two clauses, $|\chi|<1$ makes $(\ell_t,\ln x_t)$ converge
geometrically, so $\ell_t\to\ell_\infty$ with
$\mathsf{w}^{\kappa}_\infty=\gamma\theta(1-\alpha)/(1-\alpha+m)$,
$\mathsf{w}^{\varpi}_\infty=\gamma(1-\theta)(1-\alpha)/(1-\alpha+m)$ and
$\mathsf{w}^{\iota}_\infty=\alpha[\gamma\theta+m(1-\gamma\theta)/(1-\chi)]$,
all strictly positive. The class ratio then converges too, and for a
reason independent of the instruments: substituting Assumption 2 into
$\nu'=\nu g_C/g_W$ gives $\ln\nu_{t+1}=(1-\gamma)\ln\nu_t+\Theta_t$,
which is Theorem 2(i)’s own recursion with its forcing term $\Theta$
evaluated along the path; $\Theta_t$ is convergent and the map is a
contraction of modulus $1-\gamma<1$. So $x_t,z_t,\nu_t$ all converge to
finite strictly positive limits, the rest point is interior in the sense
of Assumption 4 — whose condition (A) is $g>0$ here — and $g_C$ and
$g_W$ share their limit, so Assumption 5’s two conditions coincide.
Series (29) therefore converges exactly when
$\beta e^{(1-\sigma)\ell_\infty}<1$, that is when
$\ell_\infty>\ln\beta/(\sigma-1)$; and $\ell_\infty$ is a strictly
positive combination of the same three logarithms, hence strictly
concave by Lemma 2, so the proper set is a superlevel set of a strictly
concave function and is convex. $\blacksquare$

**Corollary 5 (one unconditional closure).** *Under Proposition 16’s
hypotheses, a stationary profile evaluated from the rest point it
induces is the worker’s global best reply over his whole instrument box,
and his clause of Definition 1 closes with no hypothesis beyond
Assumptions 1–3 and 5. The best reply is unique, $\widetilde V_W$ being
strictly concave on $T$.*

*Proof.* Proposition 16 supplies Theorem 7’s hypothesis, and the rest is
Corollary 4’s argument unchanged, corner included. Strictness passes
through the strictly increasing exponential in Theorem 7’s proof.
$\blacksquare$

Three things bound what that buys, and none of them is a formality. The
face is a **face**: $\delta=\delta_G=1$ is one corner of the
depreciation rectangle Assumption 1 allows, and the cells of Computation
1 are not on it. Nothing extends by continuity, and the obstruction is
structural rather than technical — with the survival terms restored,
$\ln P_u$ is the logarithm of a sum of two log-affine terms, which is
*convex* in $(\ln x_u,\ln\iota)$ rather than affine, and (30) carries it
with a positive coefficient. That is the term (32) reports, read a date
at a time instead of in the limit, and it is why Computation 9’s
counterexample lives at interior depreciation and could not live here.
Second, the closure is the **worker’s** clause alone. The capitalist’s
rate raises the stock that funds his consumption while lowering the
consumption itself, so his date-wise object carries $\ln(1-i)$ and
$\ln\iota$ with weights this recursion does not sign, and Proposition 16
does not reach him. Third, admissibility is not free at full
depreciation: both stocks are rebuilt from nothing each period, so
Assumption 5 constrains $B$, which Assumption 2 leaves open.

**Computation 11 (the pair, measured).** *At the ten profiles of
Computation 9, with the start each incumbent induces, the per-date
Hessian of $\ell_u$ in $(\tau,\varpi)$ has a largest eigenvalue never
exceeding $1.8\times10^{-7}$ of the matrix norm, at every date $u\le399$
and every cell. That residual is reported rather than rounded to zero,
and it is identified rather than excused: it falls at $u=0$, the one
date where Proposition 15(ii)–(iii) proves the determinant is exactly
zero and a difference quotient must therefore return zero plus error,
and tripling the differencing step multiplies it by $8.97$ to $9.04$ —
the $O(h^{2})$ signature of truncation and not of a violation. The same
nine-point stencil in $(\tau,\phi)$ returns a largest eigenvalue between
$0.70$ and $0.94$ of the matrix norm at all ten: a genuine saddle, six
orders of magnitude away, which is the control that makes the first
number mean something. The cumulated Hessian of $\ln N_{W,t}$ — Theorem
7’s actual hypothesis — is negative semidefinite on the same range, and
the Hessian of $\widetilde V_W$ is negative definite at every one of the
$121$ admissible points of an $11\times11$ grid inside $T$ at each of
three cells, whose endpoints Appendix C.9 states. At the rest point of
each of the ten, criterion $(\ast\ast)$ takes values in $[0.119,0.530]$
against its threshold of $1$ and $(\ast)$ values in $[0.010,0.363]$,
with $(\ast\ast)\ge(\ast)$ at every one — which is a check on the
arithmetic and not a finding, Proposition 15(v) having proved that
ordering.*

*Unlike the elasticity of Computation 10, the tail criterion can be
swept where that computation could not, because $(\ast)$ and
$(\ast\ast)$ are closed-form at a rest point. Two-thirds of the gap
closes by derivation rather than by sweeping: neither $\mathcal{H}$ nor
$\mathcal{P}$ nor $\mathcal{G}'$ nor $\mathcal{G}''$ contains $\beta$ or
$\sigma$, so neither criterion depends on them at all except through the
admissibility gate of Proposition 6(iii). On a grid of $54{,}000$
profiles in the remaining six primitives and the three shares,
$38{,}208$ admissible after that gate ($15{,}792$ excluded improper,
none for Assumption 4), $(\ast\ast)$ **fails at $3{,}851$** and $(\ast)$
at $1{,}955$ — the joint criterion breaking on just under twice as many
cells, and on about a tenth of the admissible grid rather than in a
corner of it: the breaches occur at every one of the five $\gamma$
values and all four $\delta_G$ values swept, with worst values of $6.21$
against $2.76$. That is a refutation and stands as one. The
complementary counts establish nothing about the cells not tested. The
ordering $(\ast\ast)\ge(\ast)$ holds at all $38{,}208$, which is a check
on the implementation and not evidence, Proposition 15(v) having proved
it.*

*That tenth is not spread evenly, and where it sits is the more useful
statement. Across the twelve $(\delta,\delta_G)$ blocks the $(\ast\ast)$
failure rate runs from $0$ to $34.8\%$, and it concentrates in two
disjoint ones — $\delta=0.03$ with $\delta_G\ge0.20$, at $28.4\%$ and
$34.8\%$, and $\delta=0.15$ with $\delta_G=0.02$, at $18.4\%$ — durable
private capital against a fast-decaying public stock, or the reverse.
The other nine blocks breach at $2.3\%$ or less. It falls from $17.8\%$
at $\alpha=0.20$ to $4.1\%$ at $\alpha=0.45$, and from $21.8\%$ at
$\theta=0.3$ to $1.6\%$ at $\theta=0.8$; in $\gamma$ it barely moves at
all, $8.4\%$ to $11.5\%$. Six of the twelve blocks show no breach, but
two of those six carry only $104$ and $5$ admissible cells after the
properness gate and establish nothing. The criterion is nowhere near
vacuous on this grid: $|\mathcal{G}''|$ has median $213$ and minimum
$15.1$, the full-depreciation corner being absent from it by
construction.*

*The measure is also not a constant of the model, which the derivation
above does not say and could not. Because $\beta$ and $\sigma$ enter the
properness gate and nothing else, they cannot move a cell’s value but do
move which cells are counted. Over $\sigma\in\{1.5,2,3\}$ and
$\beta\in\{0.94,0.96,0.98\}$ the admissible count runs from $24{,}006$
to $51{,}351$ and the $(\ast\ast)$ failure rate from $2.1\%$ to
$12.8\%$, the $(\ast)$ rate from $0.01\%$ to $6.9\%$. The worst value
moves too — $6.21$ to $1.47$ — not because any cell changed but because
a tighter gate deletes the cell that attains it. So the tenth reported
above is the $(\sigma,\beta)=(2,0.96)$ figure and should be read as
one.*

*The other criterion behaves differently on the same grid, and the
contrast is the point. Taking $\tau$ to the zero of $\Xi$ and $g$ to the
rest point that zero induces — so that the growth factor is pinned by
the primitives rather than swept freely as in Computation 6 — the
down-crossing criterion (27) is refuted nowhere: it holds at all
$12{,}015$ of the $18{,}000$ cells that close, reaching at most $0.505$
of its threshold — a different grid from the $0.804$ above and not
comparable to it, $g$ being pinned here and free there. That count is
worth what its census says and no more. A third of those cells,
$4{,}001$, sit in the quadrant where $\varepsilon^{\tau}_g$ and the
bracket are both negative and the corner result does not transport, so
the zero failure rate is not the vacuous kind — though all but five of
those cells sit at $\delta=0.03$, so the coverage of that quadrant is
itself narrow. And $(\delta,\delta_G)=(0.15,0.34)$ closes at no cell and
$(0.15,0.20)$ at forty, both emptied by properness, and (27) is untested
there rather than verified. None of it touches the $\sigma=12$ cell
above, where the criterion does fail and continues to.*

*The two clauses of Definition 1 are now closed by two different
hypotheses, and since a mutual best reply needs both to hold **at the
same profile**, it is worth saying where they do. The capitalist’s
clause is closed by concavity of $\ln P_t$ in his rate; the worker’s by
joint concavity of $\ln N_{W,t}$ on $T$ — sufficient routes both, not
the conditions themselves, and Computation 13 measures what the worker’s
route gives away. These are distinct objects in distinct instruments,
neither implying the other, and each is verified — the capitalist’s by
Computation 12, on an interval of rates at the self-consistent cell, the
worker’s by the paragraph above at ten profiles over eight cells. Those
ten sit at $\tau\in\{0.34,0.45\}$ and the capitalist’s half at
$\tau=0.50$, so as reported the two share a cell and not a profile, and
the stencil was run again at the self-consistent profiles themselves.
Computation 12 reports two of them, at $\omega=0$ and at $\omega=0.50$,
and both are covered. Definition 1 fixes the start as well as the
profile, and the start each induces is the one Computation 12 evaluates
from, the two kernels agreeing on it to machine precision. There, at
$\phi=1$ exactly — the face $\varpi=\tau$ of $T$, where the stencil must
be one-sided — the cumulated Hessian of $\ln N_{W,t}$ is negative
semidefinite at every date $u\le399$. Its one zero eigenvalue falls at
$u=0$, where Proposition 15(ii)–(iii) proves the determinant vanishes
identically, and from $u=1$ the matrix is strictly definite, worst
eigenvalue $-3.0\times10^{-3}$ at $\omega=0$ and $-4.2\times10^{-3}$ at
$\omega=0.50$. The Hessian of $\widetilde V_W$ is negative definite
there and at all $121$ points of the same lattice inside $T$ from each
start. So Definition 1 is met there conditionally on a conjunction, not
on either half, and the conjunction holds at those two profiles and is
untested elsewhere. The two halves were not verified equally well, and
the weaker was the capitalist’s: Theorem 7 asks for concavity on an
interval of his rate, and Computation 9 checks the second difference at
the incumbent rate alone, which is concavity at a point. Computation 12
closes that asymmetry on the same terms the worker’s half already met.*

*A curvature certificate is a sufficient route to Definition 1 and not
the condition itself, which compares payoffs. Compared directly — the
worker’s discounted payoff over the whole instrument box and the
capitalist’s over the whole rate set, both from the start the profile
induces and with the other class’s instrument held — the incumbent is
the maximiser at both profiles. The split rests at the corner at every
admissible point sampled; each instrument’s payoff changes the sign of
its derivative exactly once, from positive to negative; and the tax and
the rate return their reported values to within the reporting lattice,
the rate agreeing besides with the closed form (12) to three decimals at
both weights by a route that shares no step with it. Two properties are
worth reporting beside the curvature. The maximiser is stable in the
horizon, identical at eight hundred dates and at twice and half that, so
it is not an artefact of truncation. And it moves by more than twenty
lattice atoms when the start is changed, which is the concept behaving
as declared rather than a fixed point being rediscovered. The region
where $(\ast\ast)$ fails is where a competing maximum would have to
live, and it carries none: sampled at a spacing an order finer than that
region is wide, its best payoff falls short of the incumbent’s by three
orders of magnitude more than the best point anywhere else in the box
does. Like the grids above this can refute and did not; it does not
establish, and nothing is claimed beyond the profiles and points
tested.*

**Computation 12 (the capitalist on an interval).** *Theorem 7’s
hypothesis for the capitalist is concavity in his rate of the cumulated
log of the object he values, and under (O) that object is $\ln P_t$
rather than $\ln N_{C,t}$ — a different function, so the question is
asked again here rather than inherited. On a $41$-point grid of the rate
spanning $[0.10,0.90]$ at the self-consistent cell, the second
difference of $\ln P_t$ in $i$ is strictly negative at every date
$t\le399$ and at every point of that grid, worst value $-1.29$; and
$V_C$ itself is strictly concave there, worst second difference
$-1.8\times10^{3}$. At $\omega=0.50$ the same holds, worst values
$-0.646$ and $-3.8\times10^{2}$. So the conjunction of Section 8.7’s two
hypotheses is verified on a grid on **both** sides at the reported cell,
and remains untested elsewhere.*

*One feature of that grid is worth separating from the verdict, because
it is a consequence of the objective rather than of the cell. All $41$
rates are admissible, where under a dynastic capitalist between $28$ and
$41$ were, the remainder improper. The reason is Assumption 5: at the
rest point each rate induces, his payoff object grows at the worker’s
factor, so his properness clause is the worker’s own read at his
exponent, and the range of rates leaving both payoffs convergent widens
accordingly. The change of objective has removed a source of
inadmissibility from the model rather than relabelled one. This is a
statement about the rest points, not about arbitrary paths: away from
one, $g_P$ and $g_W$ separate and the clause has independent content.*

*Proposition 16 is algebra and is not sampled, but its closed form is
checkable against a path built from the primitive laws, and that check
is worth reporting because it can fail. Over $18$ full-depreciation
cells spanning $\theta\in\{0.30,0.60,0.85\}$,
$\gamma\in\{0.40,0.50,0.70\}$ and two values of $B$, (39)–(40) reproduce
an independently simulated $\ell_t$ path to a worst relative
$1.1\times10^{-14}$ over $200$ dates. The **control** is the same
comparison off the face: at $\delta=\delta_G\in\{0.99,0.90,0.50,0.08\}$
the same closed form misses the simulated path by at least
$9.4\times10^{-2}$, so the first number is a verification and not an
identity restated. Eleven of the eighteen cells are admissible in the
sense of Assumption 5, which is what keeps Proposition 16 from being
vacuous; the seven that are not are improper and reported as such. The
per-date joint Hessian of $\ell_u$ in $(\tau,\varpi)$ is negative
semidefinite at those cells with a largest eigenvalue at most
$1.6\times10^{-7}$ of the matrix norm — the same $O(h^{2})$ truncation
signature Computation 11 identifies, at the same date and for the same
proved reason.*

*The proper set’s convexity is proved and not sampled, but the object it
is proved about — the closed form for $\ell_\infty$ — is checkable
against the simulator’s own admissibility verdict, which shares no step
with it. Over a $24\times24$ grid on $T$ at four values of $B$, the
closed-form gate $\ell_\infty>\ln\beta/(\sigma-1)$ and the simulated
path’s properness agree at $2{,}304$ of $2{,}304$ points, with no case
in which the capitalist’s condition bound where the worker’s did not —
which is what Proposition 16’s convergence argument predicts, the two
growth factors sharing a limit. Four thousand random chords per cell
stay inside the proper set, a check on the implementation rather than
evidence, the convexity having been proved.*

*At the counterexample cell of Computation 9 both criteria fail,
$(\ast\ast)=3.353$ against $(\ast)=2.806$, and everything breaches
earlier for the pair than for the split, as the ordering of the two
criteria requires: the date-wise condition at $u=68$ against $u=87$, and
the cumulated hypothesis at $t=504$ against $t=1190$. That last
comparison is the one worth stating plainly. The split’s counterexample
carries a discount weight of $8.0\times10^{-22}$ and the pair’s carries
$1.2\times10^{-9}$ — still negligible, but thirteen orders of magnitude
less so, and the honest reading is that the joint hypothesis is the more
exposed of the two wherever $(\ast)$ fails at all. Ten profiles and one
grid can refute joint concavity and did not; they do not establish it,
and nothing here is claimed beyond the profiles and dates tested.*

**Computation 13 (the hypothesis and the conclusion, separated at the
same points).** *Theorem 7’s per-date hypothesis is a sufficient route
to the concavity Corollary 4 uses, not the object itself, and the wedge
between the two is measurable, because the worker’s Hessian splits
exactly. On the chart $(\tau,\varpi)$, with weights
$w_t=\beta^{t}N_{W,t}^{1-\sigma}$ and per-date gradients
$a_t=\nabla\ln N_{W,t}$,*
$$\nabla^{2}\widetilde V_W \;=\; \mathsf{M}\;-\;(\sigma-1)\,\mathsf{G}, \qquad
  \mathsf{M}=\sum_t w_t\,\nabla^{2}\ln N_{W,t}, \qquad
  \mathsf{G}=\sum_t w_t\,a_t a_t^{\top}\;\succeq\;0,$$ *so the
aggregator term only helps: $\mathsf{M}$ negative semidefinite — the
cumulated form of the per-date hypothesis — implies the conclusion and
is not implied by it, and where $\mathsf{G}$ is positive definite the
conclusion is exactly the generalized-eigenvalue statement
$\lambda_{\max}(\mathsf{M},\mathsf{G})\le\sigma-1$. This is the split
Section 8.6 reads at a zero of $\Psi^{\rm fix}$ along the one direction
the crossing moves — there in its own chart $(\ln\kappa_W,\varpi)$, the
decomposition being chart-generic — now unrestricted. Measured at
$\sigma=2$, $\beta=0.96$, from the start induced by the incumbent
$(\tau,\phi,i)=(0.50,0.90,0.41)$ at each cell, on a grid crossing
$\gamma\in\{0.5,0.8,0.95,0.99\}$, $\alpha\in\{0.33,0.60,0.88\}$,
$\theta\in\{0.20,0.60,0.90\}$, the depreciation pairs $(0.08,0.05)$ and
$(0.005,0.002)$, five taxes and four splits — $1{,}246$ points evaluated
after the properness and tail gates — $\mathsf{M}$ is indefinite at $78$
of them and $\nabla^{2}\widetilde V_W$ at none: every failure of the
sufficient route lands inside the window $(0,\sigma-1]$, where the
subtracted Gram term absorbs it, with
$\min\big[(\sigma-1)-\lambda_{\max}(\mathsf{M},\mathsf{G})\big]=0.668$
over the grid and the worst point at the near-permanent pair,
$\gamma=0.99$, $\alpha=0.88$, $\theta=0.2$,
$(\delta,\delta_G)=(0.005,0.002)$. A sample refutes and cannot
establish: the census licenses no universal. What it does license is
pointed — on this grid, a failure of the per-date route says nothing
about the worker’s objective, whose curvature question it does not
answer. The pencil does.*

One exponent carries all three of the burdens above, and it is worth
saying so once. Take the worker’s $\sigma_W$, which is the one the
pencil is about. It fixes the *sign* of his felicity, which is what
makes an improper path pay $-\infty$ rather than $+\infty$, so that (A5)
binds by dominance rather than by fiat; below one he would deviate
towards the improper side, and what fails there is the equilibrium
concept rather than the arithmetic of convergence (Section 2). Its
complement $1-\sigma_W$ is the homogeneity degree carrying his state
reduction, and that reduction changes form exactly where the degree
vanishes (Section 2). And $\sigma-1$ is the threshold in the pencil
above, $\sigma$ being that same exponent at the common value Assumption
3 names. The three are one number, and it is not an elasticity read off
consumption data: his payoff object is the size of his dynasty rather
than his consumption, so no such elasticity transfers to $\sigma_W$ at
all. The capitalist’s $\sigma_C$ is not insulated in the same way, and
Section 2 says how far the transfer reaches there; the pencil, whose
$\mathsf{M}$ and $\mathsf{G}$ are built from $\ln N_{W,t}$, does not
involve it.

Read that way the pencil condition is not a regularity condition. The
Gram term is subtracted with weight $\sigma-1$, so the exponent that
keeps the equilibrium concept intact against improper paths is also what
absorbs the curvature the per-date route fails to sign. What supplies
that curvature is durability: where both stocks depreciate fully, every
date’s log growth is a nonnegative combination of logarithms of affine
coefficients and the payoff is concave outright, and it is the survival
terms that turn each such logarithm into a log-sum-exp. So
$\lambda_{\max}(\mathsf{M},\mathsf{G})\le\sigma-1$ asks that the
curvature of the worker’s felicity keep pace with the durability of the
stocks he funds — a condition on the economy rather than on the
analysis. The census above fails to refute it and, being a sample,
establishes nothing.

# 9. Related Literature

The asymmetry of objectives has a lineage of its own, and it is worth
naming because it is older than this paper’s use of it. Putting the
owned stock into the owner’s felicity, rather than valuing it only for
the consumption it later buys, is the wealth-effects specification of
Kurz (1968), revived as the “spirit of capitalism” by Zou (1994) and
taken up by Bakshi and Chen (1996) to price equities. Its usual
motivation is a preference for wealth as such. Here the motivation is
different and narrower: it is what a *class* objective has to look like
if reinvestment is to remain a decision, and the model itself supplies
the boundary. Proposition 5(i) shows the specification cannot be pushed
to its limit — an owner who values only the stock has no reinvestment
margin at all — so the interior of the family is not one choice among
many but the whole of the admissible set. That is a statement this
literature does not make, because in a representative-agent growth model
the consumption term is never in question.

Against the Lancaster (1973) lineage itself, the departure is one
primitive. In Lancaster and in Pohjola (1983) each class maximises its
own consumption stream and nothing else — no capital or wealth term
enters either payoff — and Hoel (1978) extends that analysis to the
whole Pareto set; that shared form does real work there, because it is
what makes the two-class game a game between comparable players. Section
4 records what it costs once reproduction is the objective — the
consumption ratio is identically one and no instrument reaches
distribution — and Section 6 records what replacing it costs, which at
the baseline weight is almost nothing numerically and one primitive
conceptually.

The lineage did not stop at open-loop solutions, and nothing here should
be read as supplying what it already has. Pohjola’s Stackelberg
characterisation was corrected by de Zeeuw (1992), who found a single
open-loop solution in each leadership case and left the objective values
standing. Başar, Haurie and Ricci (1985) replaced commitment to a whole
path with stagewise leadership, and showed that capitalist leadership
weakly Pareto-dominates feedback Nash — no worse for either class,
better only inside the interior of the workers’ reaction function —
while worker leadership collapses back to Nash. Haurie and Pohjola
(1987) took the game to an infinite horizon, characterised feedback Nash
and Pareto solutions, and used the Nash pair as a threat to support an
efficient equilibrium that is subgame perfect. Shimomura (1991) is the
dedicated feedback treatment. Coloma (1999) runs accumulation and income
distribution as the outcome of a dynamic game, and Leong and Huang
(2010) add uncertainty. Infinite horizons and feedback strategies have
therefore been worked in this literature for four decades, and the
departure claimed here is neither of them: it is the reproduction
object, carried in discrete time on a Nash equilibrium of the
constant-share restriction — a narrower strategy class than Markov
feedback, and one this paper does not show is without loss against
state-contingent rules.

This paper extends the size-not-condition benchmark rather than
overturning it. That result is the $\theta=1,\ \phi=0$ corner of this
model (Section 4), and it keeps binding on the reproduction metric even
once a contested social wage is present, since the composite still
equalises. What the social wage changes is not the welfare boundary but
the reach of the instruments: they now move the private-consumption
distribution and the incidence of reproduction funding. The discipline
